# Architecture-Review Dispatch Queue — the 2026-09-03 lens re-run, the five reversals, the unowned §3 items, the landscape gaps and the maintainer decisions (2026-09-03)

**What this document is.** The prioritised task queue produced from [`ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW_2026-09-02.md`](ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW_2026-09-02.md) (the review authority) after the 2026-09-03 lens re-run was appended to it as the "2026-09-03 survey" blocks under each §3 heading and §3.17. It carries one row per: every CONFIRMED or CORRECTED lens finding (synthesis Part 2, `audits/2026-09-03_architecture_lenses/SYNTHESIS.md`); the five reversals (review §4, proposed rows verbatim); every §3 item the review marks **unowned**; the landscape HIGH gaps G1–G8 (review §6.2, as pointer rows to their demonstrating targets, not re-derived); and the maintainer decisions (review §5.3 ids, the ADRs the survey touched, the ledger/queue contradictions that only a ruling resolves). Authored by lane **Eärendur** (docs class) against clone HEAD `01226c648a` (Batch 1404); base commit and gates in `LANDING_PACKET_EARENDUR.md`.

**Authority — read this before citing anything below.**

1. **This queue is a dispatch view, not a status authority.** The campaign queues, [`DEFERRED_WORK.md`](DEFERRED_WORK.md) and [`FIX_QUEUE_2026-08-27_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md`](FIX_QUEUE_2026-08-27_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md) remain the sole status authorities for every id they own; [`CAMPAIGN_STATE.md`](CAMPAIGN_STATE.md) is the tracked campaign authority. **Nothing in this document changes a row's status in any of them**, de-scores a measured red, or reopens a closed row without the evidence its row cites.
2. **This document does not launch, rule, schedule or fund a row.** The review "does not license fixes" (`ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW_PLAN_2026-09-02.md`, Phase 2). Every row here is a proposal in dispatch order; a maintainer ruling is what turns it into work, and the `Gate` column names the ruling it waits on where one exists.
3. **Premises.** Every row's `file:line` is the verifier-corrected citation from the synthesis or the review, re-derived against `main` @ `532463ae35` (Batch 1390) by the lens verifiers; the review's own §3 rows were re-derived at the same tree. A citation is a lead for the brief-writer, who re-reads it at brief-writing time (Principle 10, SR-7). **Nothing in the lens re-run was measured** — the `Measurement rows (Éowyn)` block carries the runtime leg.
4. **Owned-by.** `NEW` means the author is certain no live row tracks the item with a correct premise. `TBD-dedup (lead: <id>)` means a candidate owner was named by the synthesis or the review but not verified against the owning document by this lane — the dedup pass (Tarcil) fills the exact id or `NEW`, and flags any row whose premise a queue already refuted. A filled owner turns the row into a **pointer**, not a duplicate. **That pass has now run: no `TBD-dedup` cell remains, and §7.1 records what the fold changed.**
5. **Ids.** `AR-nnn` rows are minted here (add-only). The five reversal rows keep the review's proposed names (`NEW-…`). `AR-G1..G8` are the landscape pointers, `AR-D01..` the decisions, `AR-M01..` the measurements. The finding id (`MAB-9`, `#37`, `UP-7`, …) stays in its own column so a row resolves to the review and to the bank.

## 0. Legends

### 0.1 Priority — the rule, and no other

| Priority | Rule | Reason clause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **P0** | The ledger or docs say done/on and the code says otherwise, **or** a correctness defect on the default path. | every P0 row states which clause and the record or path |
| **P1** | A parity gap a user reaches with no owning row, **or** a blocker for named C12 / C14 / C18 / Phase-8b work. | names the surface the user reaches, or the named work |
| **P2** | Debt that taxes named planned work. | names the work it taxes |
| **P3** | Drift, hygiene, typing — **and latent parity**: a divergence no user reaches today, which P1's "a user reaches" clause excludes by construction. | names the class |

Section order: P0, then P1, P2, P3; within a priority, rows that unblock others first, then by size ascending. Severity words in the source (LOW / MEDIUM / HIGH) do **not** move a row across the rule — a LOW parity gap a user reaches is P1 and sits at the end of P1 by size.

### 0.2 Row fields

Per [`QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md`](QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md) §0.3, as columns: `Row` · `Subsystem` (review §3.n) · `Finding ids` · `Priority — reason` · `Kind` (DEFECT / DEBT / GAP / DRIFT / DECISION / MEASURE / POINTER) · `Size` (XS/S/M/L/XL) · `Tier` (**exactly one** of `SONNET-BOUNDED`, `SOL-DIRECTED`, `OPUS-JUDGMENT`, `OPUS-EDGE-EXECUTOR`, `OPUS-REVIEW`, per §0.1 there — `—` on a POINTER row, which carries no executor of its own; every engineering row additionally owes a separate OPUS-REVIEW dispatch before landing — a standing obligation not restated per row) · `Wave` (per §0.4 there: **1** ruling-free / measurement-first / dispatchable; **2** engine rows behind a measurement; **3** rows behind a maintainer answer; **4** beyond upstream) · `Backends` · `Depends on` · `Gate` (maintainer-decision id or `none` — every row carries it) · `Acceptance` (the observable behaviour, or the measurement with its multi-metric set — never the implementation shape) · `Binds` (SR ids from §0.2 there) · `Owned-by` · `Source` (`SYN` = synthesis Part 2 heading; `REV` = review §3.n row; `G` = review §6.2).

**Defaults, stated once.** `Disposition` for every row is **PROPOSED — not launched**; `Ruling touched` is **none** unless the row's `Gate` names one. `Backends` **WebGPU** unless stated. Every engine or shader row binds **SR-1, SR-2, SR-3, SR-6, SR-7, SR-8** and carries the full proof bar (behaviour spec + inertness mutant + separate review + the named Edge leg, R-2026-08-29-1); the `Binds` column lists only additions. Docs rows carry review plus one capture where visual, no spec (R-2026-08-29-1). Tools rows carry a spec only where there is logic worth pinning **and** an npm runner home.

### 0.3 What the acceptance column refuses

No row's acceptance is a source-text shape ("the cache no longer stores `undefined`"). It is a behaviour a probe or spec observes, or a number a measurement produces with its noise behaviour stated (SR-8). Where a probe can measure the feature directly, the probe is the acceptance (R-2026-08-29-1). Fixes for parity rows are captured on **both** backends; the WebGL capture must be byte-identical before and after unless the row says WebGL changes.

## Summary

A row whose source kind is compound (e.g. `DEFECT + GAP`) is counted under its first word.

Batch 1420 (lane Emeldir), reviewer Dagnir; probe round 2 Batch 1427, probe round 3 Batch 1428 (lane Emeldir).** P0 — correctness defect on the default resize path with any effect enabled: seven of eleven post-process effects are dropped permanently by `resize()` or an HDR toggle and cannot be revived; the class was fixed for TAA only (`NEW-TAA-EFFECT-NEVER-ADDED`) | DEFECT | M | OPUS-JUDGMENT | 1 | WebGPU | — | none | `AR-M06`: captures before and after `viewer.resize()` with Bloom + AO + DoF enabled differ by < 0.5 % and `pipeline.bloomEffect !== null` after the resize; the same across a `highDynamicRange` toggle; the ten dead `?.resize()` calls are either reached (in-place resize, compile count per resize stated) or removed with `LIGHT-6`'s decision recorded in the row; the three false live-slot comments are gone. **Edge leg attempted, NOT RUN — probe defect, not a product finding (Éowyn job 6, 2026-09-05):** `node Tools/visual-regression/probe-postprocess-resize-survival.mjs` exited 2 with `ReferenceError: Cesium is not defined`, thrown in the **first** `page.evaluate` (`enableEffects`, probe `:349`), before any capture, resize or slot read. **Mechanism:** the probe navigates to `Apps/CesiumViewer/index.html?renderer=webgpu` (`:473`), an ES-module page that publishes `window.viewer` and `window.CesiumDebug` but **not** `window.Cesium` (a repo-wide grep of `Apps/CesiumViewer` for `window.Cesium` returns exactly one hit and it is a comment); the probe uses the bare `Cesium` global at four call sites (`:355`, `:359`, `:360`, `:368`) and carries zero `window.Cesium` references of its own, unlike sibling runtime probes on the same page shape (e.g. `probe-ar002-per-tile-credits.mjs:106`). Consistent with the lane's own packet heading: "I wrote it, I did **not** run it." **`AR-009`'s acceptance is still uncomputed — a fix round resolving the namespace in-page (`window.Cesium`, or a dynamic import of `/Build/CesiumUnminified/index.js`) is in flight at the four call sites; this row states the defect, not a new id.** **Two probe rounds followed, both instrument defects, not product defects — the row's own claim (all eleven slots stay live across a resize and an HDR toggle) is what finally measured green.** **Round 2 (Batch 1427):** the namespace was resolved via a same-URL dynamic import of `/Build/CesiumUnminified/index.js` (identity-equal to the module the running viewer already uses) at the four call sites, plus a second, unreported defect fixed in the same patch — `slotsNow()` read `scene.context?._sceneRenderer?._postProcess`, a field declared nowhere in the tree; the real chain is `scene._alternateSceneRenderer?._postProcess`. Left uncaught, that defect would have reported every slot `false` on both legs and recorded this row's correct engine fix as a product failure. **Edge leg RE-RUN (Éowyn job 7, 2026-09-05): RED** — the `hdr` clause measured **97.288 %** against a < 0.5 % gate. **Round 3 (Batch 1428) found this RED to be the probe's own second instrument defect:** every settle called `scene.render()` on a fixed frame count with **no `scene.requestRender()` and no readiness predicate** — `Scene.js:2698-2723`'s `renderReady` docstring names the exact consequence, and the three pre-toggle captures (`resize-before`/`resize-after`/`hdr-before`) were one byte-identical file (85.5 % black, no terrain), so the resize clause's 0.000 % PASS compared a frame with a copy of itself and the `hdr` clause's 97.288 % was terrain *arriving* between an undrawn and a drawn frame, not an effect vanishing. Fixed with `settleReady()` (`globe.tilesLoaded` **and** a non-empty command list **and** `scene.renderReady`, held 8 consecutive frames, requesting a render each iteration — the fleet's own cold-start predicate, not invented a third time) plus two pure refusal decisions (`render-ready-timeout`, `contentless-capture`, floor 0.25) that raise a `ProbeRefusal` before any clause is scored rather than letting a contentless frame pass or fail. **Edge leg MET (Éowyn job 8, 2026-09-05):** all six settles reached readiness (`tilesLoaded`/`renderReady` true, `commandsDeferred` 0 at every one); both clauses **0.000 %** on real content (`nonBlackRatio` 1.0, `meanLum` 84.256 on all four captures); Bloom, AO, DoF, SunHalo and SunBloom read live at every sample point on both legs; **17 render pipelines / 16 shader modules across one resize** — the measured cost of the `LIGHT-6` removal-not-in-place-resize decision. **Row status: LANDED-MET.** An instrument gap surfaced, not fixed here: the probe never calls `armWebGPUDevices`, so its `errors[]` covers console output only, not the uncaptured-GPU-error channel — filed as `DX-59` in `QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md`. **Landed count, derived independently, not assumed:** of the eleven slots `initialize()` nulls, **7 of 11 CONFIRMED dropped** by a sticky `cache.*Initialized` latch (Bloom, AmbientOcclusion, DepthOfField, GodRay, HeatShimmer, ColdOptics, AerialPerspective) and **4 REFUTED as defects** — TAA, MotionBlur, SunHalo and SunBloom already used a live-slot gate and revive correctly; three false live-slot comments (HeatShimmer/ColdOptics/AerialPerspective) corrected, one true one (SunHalo/SunBloom) left alone. **`LIGHT-6` decision: removal, not in-place resize** — every effect's `resize()` already calls `initialize()` → `_createPipelines()`, and `createFullscreenPipeline` consults neither shader-module nor pipeline cache, so both options cost the *same* 11 shader modules + 11 render pipelines per resize at the acceptance configuration (Bloom+AO+DoF); An inertness mutant in any one of the three flips its leg back. The two planning docs are corrected in the same landing; WebGL captures byte-identical. **Edge leg attempted, NOT RUN — probe defect, not a product finding (Éowyn job 6, 2026-09-05):** `node Tools/visual-regression/probe-feature-id-texture.mjs` exited 3 (REFUSAL — `verdicts: []`, neither `cross-source` nor `key-span` reported), correctly per the probe's own contract (an exit 3 is a staging/precondition refusal, not a red). **Mechanism:** the `key-span` cell seeds `context._nextPickColor[0]` before each pick, but the two staged points meant to differ only above bit 23 came back as `0x3` vs `0x4` — consecutive small integers, differing in the LOW bits — because the probe's warm pick (`doPick(512, 384, 900, 700)`, a rectangle spanning x 62-962 / y 34-734 of a 1024×768 canvas) covers the three staged points it is meant to exclude and materialises their ids (3, 4, 5) in creation order before the first seed is written. The probe refused correctly rather than emit a verdict over an un-staged pair (which would have false-greened: 0x3 vs 0x4 differ in the low bits and would recolor differently even under the pre-fix 24-bit decode). **`AR-751`'s acceptance is still uncomputed — a fix round to the probe's staging/warm-pick sequencing is in flight; this row states the defect, not a new id.** **Consequence re-scoped at landing:** `PickId.normalizedRgba` has **zero consumers** anywhere in the tree (producers source `pickId.color` instead), so the truncation this row fixes was **live only in `FeatureIdResolve.wgsl`**; the `PickId.js` and `GraphicsContext.ts` fixes close latent traps, not an active symptom — see `AR-136`, amended below, for what `normalizedRgba` still needs. Filed as a direct consequence of review (Arachon), not fixed here: `NEW-WEBGPU-PICK-TARGET-BLEND` (`AR-891` below) — the point and polyline pick renderers keep alpha blending on their pick target, which multiplies an alpha-0 pick key away against the clear. **Round 2 (Batch 1426): a second, independent engine defect was found and fixed — the row's acceptance was mathematically unreachable without it.** `FeatureIdResolve.wgsl:64-67` at the diagnosed tip (Batch 1416; `:77-81` after this fix) emitted the low three bytes of `id * 2654435761u`; multiplication mod 2^32 propagates carries only upward, so the low 24 bits of the product depend solely on the low 24 bits of `id` regardless of decode width — Batch 1416's 32-bit decode fix was necessary but not sufficient, and survived it intact. Two symptoms, exactly the ones this row names: ids differing only above bit 23 recolour **identically**, and every multiple of 2^24 recolours to **background**. Fixed with an xorshift finalizer (`hashed ^ (hashed >> 16u)`) that folds the high half down; both symptoms are now guaranteed impossible by construction (not merely probable), since `K = 2654435761` is odd and `d ↦ d·K mod 256` is therefore a bijection. `DEFERRED_WORK.md:14561`'s heading — which had read CLOSED after Batch 1416 — is amended in place to name both root causes and was held OPEN — PENDING EDGE pending this round's own Edge leg. **Edge leg MET (Éowyn job 7, 2026-09-05):** staging landed cleanly (no `alias-pair-not-staged` refusal) with keys `0x301`/`0x1000301`/`0x3000000`; the alias pair recolours **[21,200,164]** vs **[21,121,164]**, distinct; the 2^24 multiple recolours **[0,19,0]**, non-black; all three equal the CPU hash of the full 32-bit key. **Row status: LANDED-MET.** A separate probe-staging defect (the warm pick materialising the staged ids before they could be seeded) was also fixed this round, re-derived independently of Éowyn's proposed remedy — see `LANDING_PACKET_GORLIM_R4.md` §1. Arachon's review found the operational constraint's third-clause justification mis-signed (attributes "wrong in the passing direction" to a channel-subset probe rather than to a tolerant `same()`); the correction is owed at `DEFERRED_WORK.md:14754-14761`'s next touch, not applied here (that file is outside this lane's edit scope) | SR-7 (one mutant per file) | NEW | SYN-L2 3.6; REV-L2 (a) `L2-COL-13`; round 2 + job 7: `LANDING_PACKET_GORLIM_R4.md`; `wave-p0-1-edge-2026-09-05-job7/SUMMARY.md` |

## 2. P1 — a parity gap a user reaches with no owning row, or a blocker for named C12 / C14 / C18 / Phase-8b work

Unblocks-others first (the sync cluster and the decisions), then by size. The severity word in the source does not move a row: a LOW parity gap a user reaches is P1 and sits at the end of the section.

### 2a — 2026-09-04 fold (batches L1, L2, L3 and the Éowyn runtime leg)

The bulk of this block is one family: the first `npm run test-webgpu` receipt (`ARCH-8`) returned 95 failures across 38 suites, and every one of them is an ASSERTION-class failure on a shared or WebGPU-only subject — no environment failures, no flakes, nothing skipped-as-unavailable among the 95. One row per suite, because a suite is the unit an executor can take and the unit whose green is meaningful.

Lane U opened 13 such files among the 24 conflicted `.js` (e.g. `Scene/ClippingPolygon.js`, `Scene/Scene.js`, `Renderer/ShaderBuilder.js`); git aligns a fork class method against an unrelated `X.prototype.y = function` block, so the two sides of a conflict region are frequently not about the same feature. Taking `--theirs` there reverts the ES6 conversion CLAUDE.md's own modernisation rule mandated, loses more than WebGPU code (so "re-add WebGPU code" does not restore it), and **fails silently in review** — the file still parses, exports the same symbols and passes most specs. Lane U proposes a sixth resolution class, `PORT-INTO-CLASS`: start from `ours`, read upstream's `base → theirs` diff for the semantic delta only, hand-port it into the class body. **Acceptance:** the procedure names the class-shape exception, and a mechanical guard asserts that no file which was an ES6 class at `01226c648a` is prototype-based after a merge is resolved — the invariant is genuinely about source structure, so a shape assertion is the correct instrument here. ## 6. Measurement rows (Éowyn)

Every item is CONFIRMED by source and derived, never measured (synthesis Part 5.3; review §7.5). Tier is `OPUS-EDGE-EXECUTOR` for all; Wave 1 (measurement-first). lead** — every number below was read from the receipt itself, and where a receipt's own prose
disagrees with its table, the table governs and the disagreement is recorded.

**The population was 37 at this section's original count (job 2/3), split 4 RAN (one PARTIAL) / 33 NOT RUN; `AR-M14` has since RAN and PASSED (Éowyn job 6, 2026-09-05, wave P0-1's `AR-002` acceptance) — 5 RAN / 32 NOT RUN as of this queue's current state.** `AR-M01`..`AR-M34` are

(§6a). Two counts that circulate and are wrong: the job's own summary line reads "4 rows RAN, 30 NOT
RUN" over a 34-row table showing 3 numbers and 31 NOT RUNs, and the `ARCH-8` receipt's

26 specs quoted downstream — an ~80 % over-statement of WebGPU-side breakage.

different problem with a different fix: the blocker is instrument construction, so the queue carries

#### The ten instrument families

The 31 NOT RUN §6 rows plus `AR-M22`'s denominator, `AR-M35`'s cost half, `AR-M36` and `AR-M37`
are **35 measurement slots**, batched by what the receipts say the missing instrument actually is.
The family rows are `AR-835`..`AR-844` (§2a / §3a). Two preconditions ride on family rows rather
than forming families of their own: `AR-M15` needs a **built artefact** (the define-stripped
variant) and `AR-M26` an **8-core executor**.

| Family | What the instrument is | Slots it unblocks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **TF-1 per-backend capture pair** | a parameterised scene builder + dual-backend capture + pixel / mismatch analyser | `AR-M02`, `M03`, `M07`, `M11`, `M12`, `M13`, `M16`, `M17`, `M19`, `M37` (10) |
| **TF-2 post-first-frame runtime mutation, then re-observe** | write scene/viewer state *after* the first rendered frame, re-capture, and read the property back | `AR-M04` (DPR), `M06` (resize + HDR toggle), `M08` (HDR canvas output), `M09` (shadow size / cascades) (4) |
| **TF-3 pick / visibility matrix** | per-item `visible` + `pickAsync`-at-centre over a scene matrix | `AR-M01`, `M30` (2) |
| **TF-4 per-frame cost + allocation profiler** | CDP `HeapProfiler.startSampling` over N frames **with** ms/frame in the same run — the multi-metric rule makes timings and allocations one instrument, not two | `AR-M05`, `M18` (A ms/frame + allocations, B selector µs), `M31`, `M34` (4) |
| **TF-5 encoder wrapper census** | wrap `beginRenderPass` / `beginComputePass` (and `setPipeline` / `setBindGroup`) to count opens, labels, `timestampWrites` and redundant sets | `AR-M22`'s denominator (= Éowyn item 12 / `HP-01`), `M23` (2) |
| **TF-6 GPU resource-creation and write-traffic ledger** | `createBuffer` size ledger, `createComputePipeline` label ledger, `queue.writeBuffer` per-frame counter | `AR-M10`, `M24`, `M36` (3) |
| **TF-7 forced-gate GPU pass timing** | arm `CesiumDebug.gpuPassCost` with the density gate forced on (`scene.gpuCullingHint`), and against a define-stripped build variant | `AR-M15`, `M35`'s cost half (2) |
| **TF-8 worker-pool and async-lifecycle counters** | worker-thread count, idle-core fraction, pending `TaskProcessor` promises, frames-until-a-readback-consumer-observes | `AR-M25`, `M26`, `M27` (3) |
| **TF-9 malformed-document fixture harness** | hostile CZML / KML / GeoJSON / `.vctr` / `.gltf` fixtures plus `Object.prototype`, `RangeError`, fetch-count and allocation-attempt observers | `AR-M28`, `M29` (2) |
| **TF-10 in-page state and error-surface read** | read a rendered UI or state value (credit-bar count, `msaaSamples` read-back) and classify throw-vs-silent | `AR-M14`, `M20`, `M21` (3) |

Undetermined: the other 11, of which 8 are WebGPU-only and 3 (`3d-tiles-compare`, `aec-architectural-design`, `moon`) fail on both renderers. **The discriminating step this row owes:** re-read each of the 11 with the namespace probe retried to a stated deadline instead of one post-settle read, with the bucket frame's unhandled-rejection log attached, and report per id — whether the namespace appears at all and at what time, and whether any rejection preceded it. ### 9.1 Rows the L1 continuation invalidates — read before dispatching any of them

The 2026-09-03 queue **already files `R16` and `R17` as work**, and the L1 code re-derivation
dissolves most of it. This is the highest-consequence amendment in the fold: leaving these rows as
written keeps refuted work dispatchable at **P1**, which is precisely the Principle-10 failure the
batch exists to document.

### 9.3 `DM-09` — amended in the research dispatch queue, not here

`DM-09` has no row in this queue; its pointer row is `QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md:115`,
and that document is the seat's to edit. The amendment: **the diagnosis is now split by measurement
and is not reconciled.** Éowyn's two `E-1` legs read the stall locus in opposite directions by leg
order — forward (webgl→webgpu) **off-main-thread**, pipeline cause not licensed (31.8 s gap, 66.0 %
share, 120 polls at 95 % coverage); reverse (webgpu→webgl) **MAIN-THREAD-BLOCKED**, pipeline cause
licensed (37.7 s gap, 57.7 % share, **0 of 150** predicted polls). The reverse leg **refutes** the
"four off-main-thread waits" diagnosis on the same engine and served bundle, and the forward
`ao-off` control also reads main-thread-blocked, so the split is not purely an order effect.
**`E-2` did not resolve it and cannot as built**: the trace bridged on `baseline/webgl` (residual
23 ms) and `one-tileset` (17 ms) but on **none** of the six WebGPU eight-tileset legs in either
order (`clock-sync-anchors-disagree`, residuals 308-340 ms), so **no occupant is named** and the
row's own funding gate — "the Edge run names the occupant before any fix is funded" — is not
satisfiable by this instrument until the clock bridge is fixed. Per Principle 9 the missing piece is
named rather than routed around: **a trace clock bridge that anchors within tolerance on a WebGPU
eight-tileset leg** is what `DM-09` now waits on, filed here as `AR-880` (POINTER, instrument
family TF-8). The "four off-main-thread waits" sentence is retained as *the forward leg's reading*,
not as the row's finding. The row stays HELD behind `Q-143`; **its status does not change.**


### 9.4 Two proposed rows withdrawn as duplicates (dedup pass, Grithnir, 2026-09-04)

The dedup pass found **no premise-refuted row** among the 142: no status authority's own content
contradicts a proposed row's premise. It found the next class instead — **two rows that are the same
finding filed twice**, in each case duplicating a row this queue already carried from 2026-09-03. Both
proposed rows are **withdrawn**; what each genuinely added is folded into the owning row as an
amendment, which is the house style for a fold that sharpens rather than discovers.

| Withdrawn | Owning row | What the owner keeps, and what the withdrawal adds to it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `AR-800` (L3) | `AR-024` (§3.5, `MAB-2`, P1) | Same mechanism, same file, same `DP-H2`-says-FIXED contradiction — `AR-024` already reads "`billboard.alignedAxis` is packed and read by no WGSL; the comment says the follow-up landed" at `WebGPUBillboardRenderer.js:224-228` against `AR-800`'s `:230-234`. **Amend `AR-024` with three things it does not have.** (1) **Scope**: the SDF label path and the pick pass drop `.zw` too, so all **three** consumers are affected, and the acceptance needs a three-consumer capture. (2) **The fix shape**: the renderer writes `alignedAxis.x`/`.y` and **never `.z`** (`:233-234`), and every WGSL read of `compressedAttr0` is `.xy` (`BillboardCollection.wgsl:319`, `:720`; `…Pick.wgsl:159`; `…SDF.wgsl:236`, `:589`) with `.zw` returning nothing, while WebGL consumes it at `BillboardCollectionVS.glsl:60-70` — **so the fix must widen the pack, not merely add a shader read**, and an acceptance that only asserts a shader reads the axis would pass over a still-broken two-component pack. (3) **Priority**: `AR-800` argued **P0** on the record-contradicts-code clause (`DP-H2` marked "FIXED 2026-04-16 (Batch 3)" while its own body defers shader consumption). `AR-024` sits at P1. **The re-pricing is the seat's call**, and is flagged here rather than applied. |
| `AR-776` (L2, finding `L2-COL-9`) | `AR-139` (§3.11, `1390-4 [data]`, P3) | Same dispatcher, same missing-limit-check defect, same acceptance shape. **`L2-COL-9` is therefore carried by `AR-139`**, not by a row of its own — the id is recorded here so it stays findable from this queue. **Amend `AR-139` with `AR-776`'s sharper evidence**: the method is `WebGPUEntityClusterDispatcher._ensureResources` (`:128`), and the caller's failure branch at `:258` is **structurally unreachable** — both of the method's exits are `return true` (`:131`, `:232`) and there is no `return false` anywhere in it. That converts `AR-139`'s generic "no limit check" into a proof that the error path cannot fire, which is what its acceptance (one permanent `console.error` plus a retry on the next frame) has to overcome. A near-match at `QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md:142` (`MS-04`) was checked and ruled a **false** match — adapter-limit negotiation, not a dispatcher buffer-size guard. |

Withdrawing the two rows leaves the fold at **140 new rows** and the queue at **376**. The station-3 fix round then added one more decision row (`AR-D24`, §5a — the §0.1 priority-rule gap), so the fold lands at **141 new rows** and the queue at **377**. `AR-045` is RETIRED in place and still counted: ids are add-only.

## 8. Provenance, and what this queue does not do

- **Inputs, read in full by this lane:** `ARCHITECTURE_REVIEW_2026-09-02.md` at clone HEAD `01226c648a` (§3.1-3.16, §4, §5.3, §6, §7, §8); `cesium-webgpu-worker-archive/arch-lenses-2026-09-03/SYNTHESIS.md` (Parts 1-5); `landscape-salvage/SALVAGE_TABLE.md` and `INDEX.json` for the G-row census ids; `QUEUE_2026-08-29_RESEARCH_DISPATCH.md` §0.1-0.4 for the tier, field and wave conventions; `FORK_ARCHITECTURE_REMEDIATION_PLAN_2026-07-13.md:173-235` for the ADR texts. The eleven `*.bank.md` files were **not** re-read by this lane — the synthesis is the verified register and a bank's citation is a lead, the verifier's VERDICT the premise.
- **Counts.** The summary tables at the head are generated from the rows by `_lane-out/build-queue.mjs` at authoring time; re-run it after editing rows.
- **This queue does not:** change a status in any authority; de-score a measured red; reopen a closed row; launch, rule, schedule or fund anything; renumber or reuse an id (SR-16); assign an owner it has not verified (`TBD-dedup` was the honest state until the dedup pass filled it; every filled cell names the document and line the fill rests on).
- **Tier-3 passes: run, and folded in.** *Tarcil (dedup)* — 236 rows grepped against the four doc groups: 7 owned by an exact id with a correct premise, 15 `PARTIAL: <id>`, 39 internal pointers or measurement rows with no external owner to find, 175 NEW; 4 refuted-premise flags, 5 owner corrections, 3 status-authority contradictions. Bank: `_lane-out/dedup-tarcil.md`. *Vëantur (citations)* — **977 citations: 823 open-and-support / 0 open-but-do-not-support / 89 line-drifted (swept to HEAD here) / 65 not openable at HEAD** (51 upstream-only, 12 gitignored build outputs, 2 missing docs); the ten highest-consequence rows re-derived end to end, all CONFIRMED; two wording nits fixed. Bank: `_lane-out/citations-veantur.md`. §7.1 above records what the fold changed. The citation pass was then **re-dispatched (2026-09-04) against the already-swept document** and re-derived independently: 417 distinct tokens structurally, ~90 opened directly, every citation in fourteen of the twenty-one §1 P0 rows end to end (`AR-001`–`AR-009` plus the five reversal rows) and the other seven by sample — **0 unsupported, 0 remaining drift**, which confirms the sweep landed and closes it. That second pass is authoritative for the document as it now stands; its bank’s §1–§5 “moved” list describes the pre-fold state and must not be re-applied. Two author-abbreviated filenames it resolved are corrected in the survey blocks.
- **Original handoff, as written.** *Tarcil (dedup):* for every row grep `QUEUE_*.md`, `FIX_QUEUE_2026-08-27_AUDIT_FINDINGS.md`, `DEFERRED_WORK.md`, `CAMPAIGN_STATE.md`; replace `TBD-dedup (lead: …)` with the exact id or `NEW`; flag any row whose premise a queue already refuted — `AR-096` (`DM-08`) is the known case, and the four stale-green `FIX_QUEUE` rows are the known shape. *Vëantur (citations):* every `file:line` in the review's §3 survey blocks and in the P0/P1 rows must open at the clone's HEAD and support the statement; re-derive the ten highest-consequence rows end to end — the suggested ten are `AR-001`, `AR-002`, `AR-003`, `AR-004`, `AR-009`, `AR-015`, `AR-021`, `AR-022`, `AR-027`, `AR-036`.
